View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to create a mixture of substances A and B in ratio M_a:M_b
2. We have N packages, each containing a_i grams of A and b_i grams of B, costing c_i yen
3. We must use all contents of purchased packages
4. We want to minimize the total cost
5. If impossible, return -1

## Key Insights

1. If we buy packages with indices forming a subset, the total mixture will have:
   - Total A = sum of a_i for all purchased packages
   - Total B = sum of b_i for all purchased packages

2. For the mixture to have ratio M_a:M_b, we need:
   - Total A / Total B = M_a / M_b
   - This means: (Total A) * M_b = (Total B) * M_a
   - Or: M_b * (sum of a_i) = M_a * (sum of b_i)

3. Since N ≤ 40, we can't try all 2^40 combinations. However, we can use meet-in-the-middle or observe that the constraints on a_i and b_i are small (≤ 10).

4. The practical constraint is that a_i, b_i ≤ 10, and M_a, M_b ≤ 10, so the total sums won't be too large.

## Approach

Since N is only up to 40, but 2^40 is too large, we need to think about what values we can achieve.

However, notice that we only care about the ratio condition: M_b * total_A = M_a * total_B.

We can iterate through all possible subsets (2^N combinations) and check if any subset satisfies the ratio condition. Since N ≤ 40, but 2^40 is about 1 trillion, this might be too slow.

Wait - let's reconsider. The constraints say a_i, b_i ≤ 10, so the maximum sum of A or B is 40 * 10 = 400. But we don't need to iterate through all possible sums.

Actually, since N ≤ 40, but the products M_b * sum(a_i) and M_a * sum(b_i) might have limited ranges, we can try a different approach.

Given the small values (a_i, b_i ≤ 10), and N ≤ 40, a brute force over all subsets might be acceptable in PyPy/C++ but could be borderline in Python.

But let's think: 2^40 is too large. We need a better approach.

Alternative approach: since the ratio condition is M_b * (sum a_i) = M_a * (sum b_i), we can define a key for each package: the "imbalance".

For each package i, consider the value: M_b * a_i - M_a * b_i.

Then, when we combine packages, the total imbalance is the sum of individual imbalances.

For a valid combination, we need the total imbalance to be 0.

So we can use the meet-in-the-middle technique:
- Split the packages into two groups of roughly N/2 each
- Generate all possible sums of imbalances for each group
- For each sum in first group, look for -sum in second group to make total 0
- Track the minimum cost for each achievable imbalance sum

This will be O(2^N/2 * 2^N/2) = O(2^N), but with smaller constants.

Actually, 2^20 ≈ 1 million, so 2^20 * 2^20 = 2^40 which is still too big.

Wait, we're summing imbalances, and each imbalance is at most 10*10 = 100 in absolute value, and there are at most 20 packages in each group, so the total sum range is at most 20*100 = 2000.

We can use a dictionary to store the minimum cost for each imbalance sum in each half.

## Algorithm

1. For each package, calculate imbalance = M_b * a_i - M_a * b_i
2. Split packages into two groups
3. For each group, generate all possible (sum_of_imbalances, total_cost) pairs
4. For each pair in first group, look for the opposite imbalance in the second group to get total imbalance 0
5. Take the minimum cost among all valid combinations

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # Calculate imbalance for each package
    # For a valid mixture: M_b * total_A = M_a * total_B
    # So: M_b * sum(a_i) - M_a * sum(b_i) = 0
    # For package i: imbalance_i = M_b * a_i - M_a * b_i
    
    # Split packages into two halves
    mid = N // 2
    left_packages = packages[:mid]
    right_packages = packages[mid:]
    
    # Generate all possible (imbalance_sum, cost) for left group
    left_map = {}  # imbalance_sum -> min_cost
    left_map[0] = 0  # empty set has 0 imbalance and 0 cost
    
    # Generate all subsets of left group
    left_subsets = [{}]
    for i, (a, b, c) in enumerate(left_packages):
        new_subsets = []
        for imbal_sum, cost in left_subsets[0].items():
            new_imbal = imbal_sum + (M_b * a - M_a * b)
            new_cost = cost + c
            if new_imbal not in left_subsets[0]:
                left_subsets[0][new_imbal] = min(left_subsets[0].get(new_imbal, float('inf')), new_cost)
        
        # This is getting messy. Let me rewrite properly.
    
    # Let me restart the meet-in-middle properly
    left_map = {}  # balance -> min cost
    left_map[0] = 0
    
    # Generate all possible combinations for left group
    left_items = left_packages
    # Use iterative generation
    for a, b, c in left_items:
        new_map = left_map.copy()
        for imbal, cost in left_map.items():
            new_imbal = imbal + (M_b * a - M_a * b)
            new_cost = cost + c
            if new_imbal not in new_map or new_map[new_imbal] > new_cost:
                new_map[new_imbal] = new_cost
        left_map = new_map
    
    # Generate all possible combinations for right group
    right_map = {}
    right_map[0] = 0
    
    for a, b, c in right_packages:
        new_map = right_map.copy()
        for imbal, cost in right_map.items():
            new_imbal = imbal + (M_b * a - M_a * b)
            new_cost = cost + c
            if new_imbal not in new_map or new_map[new_imbal] > new_cost:
                new_map[new_imbal] = new_cost
        right_map = new_map
    
    # Find minimum cost where left_imbal + right_imbal = 0
    min_cost = float('inf')
    for imbal_left, cost_left in left_map.items():
        target = -imbal_left
        if target in right_map:
            min_cost = min(min_cost, cost_left + right_map[target])
    
    if min_cost == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
    else:
        print(min_cost)

solve()
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to create a mixture of substances A and B in ratio M_a:M_b
2. We have N packages, each containing a_i grams of A and b_i grams of B, costing c_i yen
3. We must use all contents of purchased packages
4. We want to minimize the total cost
5. If impossible, return -1

## Key Insights

1. If we purchase packages with indices i₁, i₂, ..., iₖ, the total amount of A will be Σaᵢ and total amount of B will be Σbᵢ
2. For the mixture to have ratio M_a:M_b, we need: (Σaᵢ) / (Σbᵢ) = M_a / M_b
3. This means: (Σaᵢ) × M_b = (Σbᵢ) × M_a
4. Or: Σ(aᵢ × M_b) = Σ(bᵢ × M_a)

## Solution Approach

This is essentially a subset selection problem where we need to find a subset of packages such that the weighted sum of aᵢ×M_b equals the weighted sum of bᵢ×M_a.

Since N ≤ 40, we can't use brute force (2^40 is too large). However, we can use dynamic programming with state representing the difference between the two weighted sums.

Let's define:
- We want to achieve: Σ(aᵢ × M_b) - Σ(bᵢ × M_a) = 0
- For each package i, if we include it, it contributes (aᵢ × M_b) - (bᵢ × M_a) to our difference

We can use DP where dp[d] = minimum cost to achieve difference d.

## Step-by-step Algorithm

1. For each package, calculate its contribution to the difference: diff_i = a_i × M_b - b_i × M_a
2. Use dynamic programming to find minimum cost to achieve difference 0
3. The state space: the maximum possible difference
4. Since a_i, b_i ≤ 10 and M_a, M_b ≤ 10, the maximum contribution per package is at most 10×10 - 1×1 = 99
5. Maximum total difference: at most 40×99 = 3960

## Implementation

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # For each package, calculate the difference contribution and cost
    # We want: sum(a_i * M_b) = sum(b_i * M_a)
    # So we want: sum(a_i * M_b - b_i * M_a) = 0
    
    # Maximum possible difference we might see
    # Each package contributes at most max(a_i*M_b - b_i*M_a)
    # Since a_i, b_i <= 10, M_a, M_b <= 10, so max difference per package is 10*10 - 1*1 = 99
    # With N=40, total range is at most 40*99 = 3960
    
    # We'll use DP where dp[d] = minimum cost to achieve difference d
    # We need to handle negative differences too
    max_diff = 40 * 10 * 10  # 4000 is safe upper bound
    
    # We'll use a dictionary or list to store minimum cost for each difference
    # Since we have negative differences, we'll offset by max_diff
    dp = {0: 0}  # difference 0 has cost 0
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        diff_contribution = a * M_b - b * M_a
        
        for current_diff, current_cost in dp.items():
            new_diff = current_diff + diff_contribution
            new_cost = current_cost + c
            
            # Update new_dp with the minimum cost for new_diff
            if new_diff not in new_dp or new_dp[new_diff] > new_cost:
                new_dp[new_diff] = new_cost
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    # Check if we can achieve difference 0
    if 0 in dp:
        print(dp[0])
    else:
        print(-1)

solve()
```

Wait, let me verify this with the sample:
- N=3, M_a=1, M_b=1
- Package 1: a=1, b=2, c=1 → diff = 1*1 - 2*1 = -1
- Package 2: a=2, b=1, c=2 → diff = 2*1 - 1*1 = 1  
- Package 3: a=3, b=3, c=10 → diff = 3*1 - 3*1 = 0

We want to find subset with total diff = 0.

Possible combinations:
- Empty set: diff=0, cost=0 (but we need to produce substance C, so probably need at least one package?)
- Package 3: diff=0, cost=10
- Packages 1+2: diff = -1+1 = 0, cost = 1+2 = 3

So minimum cost is 3. This matches the expected output.

The algorithm works correctly.

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # For each package, calculate the difference contribution to the target ratio
    # We want sum(a_i * M_b) = sum(b_i * M_a)
    # So we want sum(a_i * M_b - b_i * M_a) = 0
    
    # Use DP to find minimum cost to achieve difference 0
    # dp[d] = minimum cost to achieve difference d
    dp = {0: 0}  # Initially, difference 0 with cost 0
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        # Create a new state dictionary to avoid using updated values in same iteration
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        diff_contribution = a * M_b - b * M_a
        
        # For each existing state, try adding current package
        for current_diff, current_cost in dp.items():
            new_diff = current_diff + diff_contribution
            new_cost = current_cost + c
            
            # Update new_dp with minimum cost for new_diff
            if new_diff not in new_dp or new_dp[new_diff] > new_cost:
                new_dp[new_diff] = new_cost
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    # If we can achieve difference 0, return the cost, otherwise -1
    if 0 in dp:
        print(dp[0])
    else:
        print(-1)

solve()
```

def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # For each package, calculate the difference contribution to the target ratio
    # We want sum(a_i * M_b) = sum(b_i * M_a)
    # So we want sum(a_i * M_b - b_i * M_a) = 0
    
    # Use DP to find minimum cost to achieve difference 0
    # dp[d] = minimum cost to achieve difference d
    dp = {0: 0}  # Initially, difference 0 with cost 0
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        # Create a new state dictionary to avoid using updated values in same iteration
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        diff_contribution = a * M_b - b * M_a
        
        # For each existing state, try adding current package
        for current_diff, current_cost in dp.items():
            new_diff = current_diff + diff_contribution
            new_cost = current_cost + c
            
            # Update new_dp with minimum cost for new_diff
            if new_diff not in new_dp or new_dp[new_diff] > new_cost:
                new_dp[new_diff] = new_cost
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    # If we can achieve difference 0, return the cost, otherwise -1
    if 0 in dp:
        print(dp[0])
    else:
        print(-1)

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. Dolphin needs to create a mixture of substances A and B in ratio M_a:M_b
2. He can buy packages of chemicals, each containing a_i grams of A and b_i grams of B
3. Each package costs c_i yen
4. He must use all contents of purchased packages
5. We need to find the minimum cost to achieve the desired ratio, or -1 if impossible

## Key Insights

1. When we combine packages, the total A amount is the sum of a_i's, and total B amount is the sum of b_i's
2. For the mixture to have ratio M_a:M_b, we need: (total A) / (total B) = M_a / M_b
3. This means: total A × M_b = total B × M_a
4. So: sum(a_i) × M_b = sum(b_i) × M_a
5. This can be rewritten as: sum(a_i × M_b - b_i × M_a) = 0

## Solution Approach

This is essentially a subset sum problem where we want to find a subset of packages such that:
- sum(a_i × M_b - b_i × M_a) = 0
- The total cost is minimized

The key insight is that we can transform the problem:
- For each package i, define a weighted value w_i = a_i × M_b - b_i × M_a
- We want to find a subset where sum(w_i) = 0
- Among all such subsets, we want the one with minimum total cost

Since N ≤ 40, a brute force approach with 2^40 possibilities would be too expensive. However, since the values are small (a_i, b_i ≤ 10, M_a, M_b ≤ 10), the weighted sum won't be too large.

Let's calculate the range of possible weighted sums:
- Each w_i ranges from -100 to +100 (since a_i × M_b ≤ 10×10 = 100, b_i × M_a ≤ 10×10 = 100)
- Maximum possible absolute sum is 40 × 100 = 4000

But since we're looking for sum exactly 0, we can use dynamic programming with the possible weighted sums.

## Dynamic Programming Approach

We'll use DP where:
- dp[s] = minimum cost to achieve weighted sum s
- Initially: dp[0] = 0 (no packages chosen)
- For each package, update the DP table by considering adding that package

Since the weighted sum can be negative, we'll offset it by adding a base value (like the maximum possible absolute sum or a known value).

Actually, let's reconsider the range. The maximum absolute value of w_i is 100, and with N=40, the maximum absolute total sum is 4000. This is too large for a straightforward DP.

But notice that we're looking for sum=0, and the values are relatively small. However, with N=40, we might need a more efficient approach.

Wait, let me think about this more systematically. The constraint that gcd(M_a,M_b)=1 suggests the ratio is in lowest terms, but that doesn't directly help with the subset sum.

Given the small constraints on a_i, b_i (≤10), and the fact that we need sum(a_i × M_b - b_i × M_a) = 0, let's calculate the possible range.

Each w_i = a_i × M_b - b_i × M_a, so |w_i| ≤ 10×10 + 10×10 = 200.
With N=40, the total sum can range from -8000 to +8000.

This is still too large for standard DP with 8000 states.

But let's reconsider - since we're dealing with small values and the problem is small, maybe we can use meet-in-the-middle or just optimize for the fact that the values are small.

Actually, let's check the actual range more carefully:
- a_i ≤ 10, b_i ≤ 10
- M_a, M_b ≤ 10
- So |w_i| ≤ 10×10 + 10×10 = 200
- With N=40, |total_sum| ≤ 40×200 = 8000

Wait, actually, let me reconsider the problem. Since we need sum(a_i × M_b) = sum(b_i × M_a), this means the weighted sum is 0. 

Let me implement a DP with a reasonable offset. Since the values are small, the actual range might be smaller than expected in practice.

But with N=40 and maximum |w_i|=200, the range is 8000, which is acceptable in Python with careful implementation.

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # For each package, compute the weighted value w_i = a_i * M_b - b_i * M_a
    # We want sum of w_i to be 0
    # And minimize total cost
    
    # The range of possible sums: each w_i can be from -200 to 200
    # Maximum absolute sum is 40 * 200 = 8000
    # Use offset of 4000 to work with positive indices
    
    max_abs_sum = 40 * 200  # 8000
    offset = max_abs_sum
    
    # dp[s] = minimum cost to achieve weighted sum s
    # s ranges from -max_abs_sum to max_abs_sum
    # We'll use a dictionary or array indexed by offset
    
    # Initialize: dp[0] = 0 (no packages selected)
    dp = {0: 0}
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        w = a * M_b - b * M_a
        
        for current_sum, cost in list(dp.items()):
            new_sum = current_sum + w
            new_cost = cost + c
            # Update new_dp with the minimum cost for new_sum
            if new_sum in new_dp:
                new_dp[new_sum] = min(new_dp[new_sum], new_cost)
            else:
                new_dp[new_sum] = new_cost
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    # Check if sum 0 is achievable
    if 0 in dp:
        print(dp[0])
    else:
        print(-1)

solve()
```

Wait, this approach might be too slow or consume too much memory. The DP dictionary could grow to have up to 8001 keys, and we have 40 iterations, so it's O(N * range) which is about 40 * 8000 = 320,000 operations. This should be acceptable in Python.

But let me verify the logic:

For each package, we compute w_i = a_i * M_b - b_i * M_a
We want the total sum of w_i to be 0
Each state represents a current weighted sum and the minimum cost to achieve it

This seems correct.

Let me trace through the sample:
- N=3, M_a=1, M_b=1
- Package 1: a=1, b=2, c=1 → w = 1*1 - 2*1 = -1
- Package 2: a=2, b=1, c=2 → w = 2*1 - 1*1 = 1  
- Package 3: a=3, b=3, c=10 → w = 3*1 - 3*1 = 0

Process:
- Start: dp = {0: 0}
- After package 1: dp = {0:0, -1:1}
- After package 2: 
  - 0 + 1 = 1 → cost 2
  - -1 + 1 = 0 → cost 1+2=3
  - So dp = {0:3, 1:2, -1:1}
- After package 3:
  - 0 + 0 = 0 → cost 0+10=10 (but we already have 0 at cost 3)
  - 1 + 0 = 1 → cost 2+10=12
  - -1 + 0 = -1 → cost 1+10=11
  - So dp = {0:3, 1:12, -1:11}

The minimum cost for sum=0 is 3, which matches the expected output.

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # dp[sum] = minimum cost to achieve this weighted sum
    dp = {0: 0}
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        w = a * M_b - b * M_a
        # Create new_dp as a copy of current dp
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        
        # For each existing sum in dp, try adding current package
        for current_sum, cost in dp.items():

            new_cost = cost + c

            else:
                new_dp[new_sum] = new_cost
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    if 0 in dp:
        print(dp[0])
    else:
```

You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Dolphin is planning to generate a small amount of a certain chemical substance C.

In order to generate the substance C, he must prepare a solution which is a mixture of two substances A and B in the ratio of M_a:M_b.

He does not have any stock of chemicals, however, so he will purchase some chemicals at a local pharmacy.

The pharmacy sells N kinds of chemicals. For each kind of chemical, there is exactly one package of that chemical in stock.

The package of chemical i contains a_i grams of the substance A and b_i grams of the substance B, and is sold for c_i yen (the currency of Japan).

Dolphin will purchase some of these packages. For some reason, he must use all contents of the purchased packages to generate the substance C.

Find the minimum amount of money required to generate the substance C.

If it is not possible to generate the substance C by purchasing any combination of packages at the pharmacy, report that fact.  

-----Constraints-----
 - 1≦N≦40 
 - 1≦a_i,b_i≦10 
 - 1≦c_i≦100 
 - 1≦M_a,M_b≦10 
 - gcd(M_a,M_b)=1
 - a_i, b_i, c_i, M_a and M_b are integers.

-----Input-----

a_1 b_1 c_1  
a_2 b_2 c_2
:  
a_N b_N c_N  

-----Output-----

-----Sample Input-----

1 2 1
2 1 2
3 3 10

-----Sample Output-----
3

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to create a mixture of substances A and B in ratio M_a:M_b
2. We have N packages, each containing a_i grams of A and b_i grams of B, costing c_i yen
3. We must use all contents of purchased packages
4. Goal: Find minimum cost to achieve the required ratio, or -1 if impossible

## Key Insights

1. If we buy packages with indices in a set S, the total A = Σ(a_i) for i in S, and total B = Σ(b_i) for i in S
2. For the ratio to be M_a:M_b, we need: (total A) / (total B) = M_a / M_b
3. This means: total A × M_b = total B × M_a
4. So: M_b × Σ(a_i) = M_a × Σ(b_i)
5. This can be rewritten as: Σ(M_b × a_i - M_a × b_i) = 0

## Algorithm Approach

This is essentially a subset sum problem where we want to find a subset of packages such that:
- Σ(M_b × a_i - M_a × b_i) = 0
- And we minimize the total cost Σ(c_i)

Since N ≤ 40, we can't try all 2^40 subsets. However, note that the values of a_i, b_i are small (≤10), and M_a, M_b ≤ 10.

The key insight is that we're looking for a subset where the weighted sum of (M_b×a_i - M_a×b_i) equals 0.

Since the coefficients are bounded, we can use dynamic programming with a state that tracks the sum of (M_b×a_i - M_a×b_i).

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. For each package i, calculate the "balance" = M_b × a_i - M_a × b_i
2. For each package, calculate the cost
3. Use dynamic programming to find all possible sums of balances and their minimum costs
4. If sum 0 is achievable, return the minimum cost; otherwise return -1

## Implementation Details

- Use DP where dp[s] = minimum cost to achieve balance s
- Initialize dp[0] = 0 (no packages selected)
- For each package, update the DP table by considering whether to include it or not
- The balance values can be negative, so we need to handle that

Since a_i, b_i ≤ 10, and M_a, M_b ≤ 10, the balance range is at most 100 in absolute value.

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # Calculate balance for each package: M_b * a - M_a * b
    # We need sum of balances = 0 for the ratio to be M_a:M_b
    balances = []
    costs = []
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        balance = M_b * a - M_a * b
        balances.append(balance)
        costs.append(c)
    
    # DP: dp[s] = minimum cost to achieve balance s
    # We'll use a dictionary to store minimum cost for each balance
    # Since balance can be negative, we'll offset by a base value
    
    # Find range of possible balances
    min_balance = min(balances)
    max_balance = max(balances)
    
    # Use offset to work with non-negative indices
    base = -min_balance
    max_possible = max_balance - min_balance + 1
    
    # dp[i] = minimum cost to achieve balance (i - base)
    dp = [float('inf')] * (max_possible + 1)
    dp[0] = 0  # balance 0 with cost 0 (no packages)
    
    # Process each package
    for i in range(N):
        balance = balances[i]
        cost = costs[i]
        
        # We need to update dp in reverse order to avoid using same package twice
        new_dp = dp[:]
        for current_balance in range(len(dp)):
            if dp[current_balance] != float('inf'):
                new_balance = current_balance + balance
                if new_balance < 0:
                    new_balance = 0  # This shouldn't happen due to our base
                new_balance_idx = new_balance - base
                if new_balance_idx < len(new_dp):
                    new_dp[new_balance_idx] = min(new_dp[new_balance_idx], dp[current_balance] + cost)
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    # Check if balance 0 is achievable
    if dp[0] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
    else:
        print(dp[0])

solve()
```

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to create a mixture of substances A and B in ratio M_a:M_b
2. We have N packages, each containing a_i grams of A and b_i grams of B, costing c_i yen
3. We must use all contents of purchased packages
4. We want to minimize the total cost
5. If impossible, return -1

## Key Insights

1. When we combine packages, the total A grams = sum of a_i for all selected packages
2. The total B grams = sum of b_i for all selected packages
3. For the mixture to have ratio M_a:M_b, we need: total_A / total_B = M_a / M_b
4. This means: total_A * M_b = total_B * M_a
5. We can rewrite this as: total_A * M_b - total_B * M_a = 0

## Approach

This is a subset sum problem with a linear constraint. Since N ≤ 40, we can't try all 2^40 subsets directly.

However, since the constraints on a_i, b_i are small (≤ 10) and we only have 40 items, we can use dynamic programming where the state is (total_A, total_B).

But the total possible A and B values could be up to 400 (40×10), which is manageable.

Actually, since we only care about the ratio constraint, we can work with the equation:
total_A * M_b = total_B * M_a

Let's define a DP approach where:
- dp[A][B] = minimum cost to achieve A grams of A and B grams of B
- We need to find if there exists any state where A * M_b = B * M_a

But the range of A and B could be large. Let's think differently.

Since we need A * M_b = B * M_a, we can think in terms of the "balanced" units:
- Any valid solution must have A * M_b = B * M_a = k (for some k)
- So A = k / M_b and B = k / M_a

But since A and B must be integers, k must be divisible by both M_a and M_b.

Let's define k = LCM(M_a, M_b) * t for some integer t ≥ 1.

Then A = (LCM(M_a, M_b) * t) / M_b = (M_a * t) / gcd(M_a, M_b) = (M_a / gcd(M_a, M_b)) * t
Similarly, B = (M_a * t) / M_a = t

Wait, let me recalculate:
- A * M_b = B * M_a
- So A / B = M_a / M_b
- For integers A and B, A = M_a * k, B = M_b * k for some positive integer k

This is simpler! So we need to find if we can achieve total_A = M_a * k and total_B = M_b * k for some positive integer k.

## Algorithm

1. Use dynamic programming where dp[A][B] = minimum cost to achieve A grams of A and B grams of B
2. Initialize dp[0][0] = 0, all others = infinity
3. For each package, update the DP table
4. After processing all packages, check all states where A = M_a * k and B = M_b * k for some k
5. Return the minimum cost among all valid states, or -1 if none exist

The maximum possible k: since each a_i ≤ 10 and there are at most 40 packages, maximum total A is 400, so maximum k is 400 / M_a.

Let me implement this with the constraint that A and B can be up to 400.

```python
import sys
from math import gcd

def solve():
    # Read input
    line = input().split()
    N = int(line[0])
    M_a = int(line[1])
    M_b = int(line[2])
    
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # Maximum possible total A or B
    max_total = 400  # since 40 * 10 = 400
    
    # dp[a][b] = minimum cost to achieve a grams of A and b grams of B
    # Initialize with infinity
    INF = float('inf')
    dp = [[INF] * (max_total + 1) for _ in range(max_total + 1)]
    dp[0][0] = 0
    
    # Process each package
    for a_i, b_i, c_i in packages:
        # We need to update dp backwards to avoid using the same package twice
        new_dp = [row[:] for row in dp]
        
        for total_a in range(max_total + 1):
            for total_b in range(max_total + 1):
                if dp[total_a][total_b] != INF:
                    new_a = total_a + a_i
                    new_b = total_b + b_i
                    if new_a <= max_total and new_b <= max_total:
                        new_dp[new_a][new_b] = min(new_dp[new_a][new_b], dp[total_a][total_b] + c_i)
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    # Find minimum cost for valid states
    min_cost = INF
    
    # Check all possible k such that total_A = M_a * k and total_B = M_b * k
    # k ranges from 1 to max_total // M_a
    max_k = max_total // M_a
    
    for k in range(1, max_k + 1):
        total_A = M_a * k
        total_B = M_b * k
        
        if total_A <= max_total and total_B <= max_total:
            if dp[total_A][total_B] != INF:
                min_cost = min(min_cost, dp[total_A][total_B])
    
    if min_cost == INF:
        print(-1)
    else:
        print(min_cost)
```

Wait, I need to be more careful about the DP update. I should update the DP in reverse order to avoid using the same package multiple times in one iteration.

Actually, since we're processing each package once, and we want to consider each package exactly once, we need to update the DP in a way that each package is added once.

My current approach with new_dp is correct - it's a standard knapsack style where we process each item and update the state.

- N=3, M_a=1, M_b=1
- Package 1: (1,2,1)
- Package 2: (2,1,2) 
- Package 3: (3,3,10)

We want A:B = 1:1, so A=B=k for some k.

Possible k values: k=1,2,3,...
- k=1: A=1, B=1
- k=2: A=2, B=2
- k=3: A=3, B=3

For k=3: we can get (3,3) by taking package 1 and package 2: (1+2, 2+1) = (3,3) with cost 1+2=3.

This matches the expected output.

```python
import sys
from math import gcd

def solve():
    # Read input
    line = input().split()
    N = int(line[0])
    M_a = int(line[1])
    M_b = int(line[2])
    
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # Maximum possible total A or B
    max_total = 400  # since 40 * 10 = 400
    
    # dp[a][b] = minimum cost to achieve a grams of A and b grams of B
    # Initialize with infinity
    INF = float('inf')
    dp = [[INF] * (max_total + 1) for _ in range(max_total + 1)]
    dp[0][0] = 0
    
    # Process each package
    for a_i, b_i, c_i in packages:
        # Create a copy of current dp state
        new_dp = [row[:] for row in dp]
        
        # For each current state, add this package
        for total_a in range(max_total + 1):
            for total_b in range(max_total + 1):
                if dp[total_a][total_b] != INF:
                    new_a = total_a + a_i
                    new_b = total_b + b_i
                    if new_a <= max_total and new_b <= max_total:
                        new_dp[new_a][new_b] = min(new_dp[new_a][new_b], dp[total_a][total_b] + c_i)
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    # Find minimum cost for valid states
    min_cost = INF
    
    # Check all possible k such that total_A = M_a * k and total_B = M_b * k
    max_k = max_total // M_a
    
    for k in range(1, max_k + 1):
        total_A = M_a * k
```

    # Maximum possible total A or B
    max_total = 400  # since 40 * 10 = 400

    # Initialize with infinity
    INF = float('inf')
    dp = [[INF] * (max_total + 1) for _ in range(max_total + 1)]
    dp[0][0] = 0
    
    # Process each package
    for a_i, b_i, c_i in packages:
        # Create a copy of current dp state
        new_dp = [row[:] for row in dp]

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Dolphin is planning to generate a small amount of a certain chemical substance C.

In order to generate the substance C, he must prepare a solution which is a mixture of two substances A and B in the ratio of M_a:M_b.

He does not have any stock of chemicals, however, so he will purchase some chemicals at a local pharmacy.

The pharmacy sells N kinds of chemicals. For each kind of chemical, there is exactly one package of that chemical in stock.

The package of chemical i contains a_i grams of the substance A and b_i grams of the substance B, and is sold for c_i yen (the currency of Japan).

Dolphin will purchase some of these packages. For some reason, he must use all contents of the purchased packages to generate the substance C.

Find the minimum amount of money required to generate the substance C.

If it is not possible to generate the substance C by purchasing any combination of packages at the pharmacy, report that fact.  

-----Constraints-----
 - 1≦N≦40 
 - 1≦a_i,b_i≦10 
 - 1≦c_i≦100 
 - 1≦M_a,M_b≦10 
 - gcd(M_a,M_b)=1
 - a_i, b_i, c_i, M_a and M_b are integers.

-----Input-----
The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M_a M_b  
a_1 b_1 c_1  
a_2 b_2 c_2
:  
a_N b_N c_N  

-----Output-----
Print the minimum amount of money required to generate the substance C. If it is not possible to generate the substance C, print -1 instead.

-----Sample Input-----
3 1 1
1 2 1
2 1 2
3 3 10

-----Sample Output-----
3

The amount of money spent will be minimized by purchasing the packages of chemicals 1 and 2.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to create a mixture of substances A and B in ratio M_a:M_b
2. We have N packages, each containing a_i grams of A and b_i grams of B, priced at c_i yen
3. We must use all contents of purchased packages
4. We want to minimize the total cost
5. If impossible, return -1

## Key Insights

1. If we purchase packages with indices in some subset S, the total amount of A will be Σ(a_i for i in S) and total amount of B will be Σ(b_i for i in S)
2. For the ratio to be M_a:M_b, we need: (Σa_i) / (Σb_i) = M_a / M_b
3. This is equivalent to: (Σa_i) * M_b = (Σb_i) * M_a
4. So we need: M_b * Σa_i = M_a * Σb_i
5. We can reframe this as finding a subset where the weighted sum of a_i with weight M_b balances the weighted sum of b_i with weight M_a

## Approach

This is essentially a subset sum problem where we need to find a combination that satisfies a specific equation.

Let's define the difference: for each package i, if we include it, it contributes (M_b * a_i - M_a * b_i) to our "difference" value.

If we have a total difference of 0, then M_b * (sum of a_i) = M_a * (sum of b_i), which gives us the desired ratio.

So we need to find a subset of packages whose total contribution to the difference is 0.

This is a weighted subset sum problem. Since N ≤ 40, we can't use simple DP with 2^40 states. However, since the values are small (a_i, b_i ≤ 10, M_a, M_b ≤ 10), the maximum possible sum of M_b * a_i or M_a * b_i is bounded.

Let's calculate the maximum possible sum:
- Maximum sum of M_b * a_i: 10 * 10 * 40 = 4000
- But we need to be more careful

Actually, since a_i, b_i ≤ 10, and N ≤ 40:
- Maximum sum of M_b * a_i: 10 * 10 * 40 = 4000
- Maximum sum of M_a * b_i: 10 * 10 * 40 = 4000

But we can further bound this. We only need to track the difference (M_b * sum_a - M_a * sum_b).

The maximum absolute value of this difference is at most 4000.

Since 4000 is manageable, we can use dynamic programming where dp[i][diff] = minimum cost to achieve difference 'diff' using first i packages.

However, 4000 is too large for a DP array of size 40 * 4000 = 160,000, which might be acceptable.

Actually, let me reconsider. The difference can range from -max_diff to +max_diff.

Let me calculate the maximum possible absolute difference:
- Max difference = max(M_b * Σa_i - M_a * Σb_i)
- In worst case, if all packages have large a_i and small b_i: M_b * (10 * 40) - M_a * (1 * 40) = 10 * 400 - 1 * 40 = 3960
- Similarly, worst case against would be 3960

So we need to consider a range from -3960 to +3960, which is about 8000 states. With 40 items, this is 40 * 8000 = 320,000 states, which is acceptable.

But we can optimize by noting that the actual range might be smaller.

## Algorithm

1. Read input
2. For each package, compute the contribution to difference: diff_i = M_b * a_i - M_a * b_i
3. Use dynamic programming: dp[diff] = minimum cost to achieve difference `diff`
4. Initialize dp[0] = 0 (no packages selected)
5. For each package, update dp for all possible differences
6. After processing all packages, if dp[0] exists, return dp[0], else return -1

## Implementation

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # The difference we need is 0: M_b * sum_a = M_a * sum_b
    # For each package, the contribution to difference is M_b * a - M_a * b
    # We want a subset where sum of contributions = 0
    
    # Find the range of possible differences
    # Maximum possible absolute difference
    max_sum_a = 10 * N  # each a_i <= 10
    max_sum_b = 10 * N  # each b_i <= 10
    max_diff = M_b * max_sum_a + M_a * max_sum_b
    
    # We'll use DP where dp[d] = minimum cost to achieve difference d
    # We need to consider negative differences too
    # Range from -max_diff to max_diff
    # But we can use offset: offset = max_diff
    
    offset = max_diff
    dp = {}
    dp[0] = 0  # start with no packages having cost 0
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        # For each existing difference, try adding current package
        for diff, cost in dp.items():
            new_diff = diff + (M_b * a - M_a * b)
            new_cost = cost + c
            # Update new_dp with the minimum cost for new_diff
            if new_diff not in new_dp or new_dp[new_diff] > new_cost:
                new_dp[new_diff] = new_cost
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    # Check if we can achieve difference 0
    if 0 in dp:
        print(dp[0])
    else:
        print(-1)

solve()
```

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))

    # For each package, the contribution to difference is M_b * a - M_a * b
    # We want a subset where sum of contributions = 0
```

```python
    # Start with empty set having cost of a large number (we'll use inf)
    max_diff = 2000
    offset = max_diff
    dp = {}

    dp[0] = float('inf')

            if new_diff not in new_dp or new_dp[new_diff] > new_cost:

        # Also, we can take just this package alone
        package_diff = M_b * a - M_a * b
        package_cost = c

            new_dp[package_diff] = package_cost
```

Wait, this has a bug. When I do "new_dp = dp.copy()", I'm not adding the states from the package alone.

In the loop above, I'm only adding states by adding the package to existing states.

I need to do it differently.

Actually, the standard subset sum DP is:

For each package, update new_dp from dp by considering both taking and not taking.

But in my case, I'm building it iteratively.

Let me implement the standard knapsack style:

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # The difference we need is 0: M_b * sum_a = M_a * sum_b
    # Contribution of package i: diff_i = M_b * a_i - M_a * b_i
    # We want sum of diff_i over selected packages = 0
    
    # DP: dp[diff] = minimum cost to achieve difference 'diff'
    # Start with empty set having cost inf (to exclude it)
    max_diff = 2000
    dp = {0: float('inf')}
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        # Create new_dp as a copy of current dp
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        
        # For each existing state, we can add the current package
        for diff, cost in dp.items():
            new_diff = diff + (M_b * a - M_a * b)
            new_cost = cost + c
            # Update new_dp with the minimum cost
            if new_diff not in new_dp or new_dp[new_diff] > new_cost:
                new_dp[new_diff] = new_cost
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    # After processing all packages, if we can achieve difference 0 with finite cost
    if dp.get(0, float('inf')) == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
    else:
        print(dp[0])

solve()
```

But this still doesn't include the case of taking a single package.

When I process a package, I'm only adding it to existing states, not creating a state for just this package.

For example, if I have only one package, initially dp = {0: inf}, then after processing:
- new_dp = {0: inf}
- For diff=0, new_diff = 0 + (M_b*a - M_a*b), new_cost = inf + c = inf
- So new_dp remains {0: inf}

So I never create the state for just this package.

I need to modify to include the package alone.

Standard way: when iterating, create new states from existing states with the package added, and also add the state of just the package.

But in this case, "state of just the package" is equivalent to adding it to the empty set.

The empty set has diff=0 with cost=inf.

So when I add the package to empty set, I get diff = (M_b*a - M_a*b) with cost = inf + c = inf.

So I still don't get a finite cost.

Therefore, I need to initialize dp with the empty set having cost 0, and then after processing, if the cost for diff=0 is 0, it's empty set, which is invalid.

So my only option is to initialize with empty set having cost 0, and then after the algorithm, if the minimum cost for diff=0 is 0, then either it's empty or there's a non-empty with cost 0, but cost 0 only if c_i=0, which is not possible.

Since c_i >= 1, any non-empty subset has cost >= 1.

Therefore, if there is a solution with diff=0 and non-empty, then dp[0] will be at least 1.

And the empty set has cost 0.

So in the final dp[0], if it is 0, it means only empty set works (invalid), if it is >=1, it means there is a non-empty solution.

But in the empty set case, only when sum_a=0 and sum_b=0, which is not physical, so we should return -1.

So the algorithm is:

1. Use DP with empty set having cost 0
2. After processing all packages, if dp[0] == 0, then only empty set works (invalid) → return -1
3. If dp[0] > 0, then there is a non-empty subset with diff=0 → return dp[0]

But is that true? Could there be a non-empty subset with cost 0? No, because c_i >= 1 for each package.

So any non-empty subset has cost >=1.

Therefore, if dp[0] > 0, it must be from a non-empty subset.

And if dp[0] == 0, only empty set works.

So I can do:

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # The difference we need is 0: M_b * sum_a = M_a * sum_b
    # For each package, the contribution to difference is M_b * a - M_a * b
    # We want a subset where sum of contributions = 0
    
    # DP: dp[diff] = minimum cost to achieve difference 'diff'
    # Start with empty set having cost 0
    # Maximum possible absolute difference
    max_val = 2000
    dp = {0: 0}
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        
        # For each existing state, we can add the current package
        for diff, cost in dp.items():
            new_diff = diff + (M_b * a - M_a * b)
            new_cost = cost + c
            if new_diff not in new_dp or new_dp[new_diff] > new_cost:
                new_dp[new_diff] = new_cost
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    # If we can achieve difference 0 with cost > 0, then we have a valid non-empty solution
    if dp.get(0, float('inf')) > 0:
        print(dp[0])
    else:
        print(-1)

solve()
```

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # The difference we need is 0: M_b * sum_a = M_a * sum_b
    # Contribution of package i: diff_i = M_b * a_i - M_a * b_i
    # We want sum of diff_i over selected packages = 0
    
    # DP: dp[diff] = minimum cost to achieve difference 'diff'
    # Start with empty set having cost of a large number (since we don't want it)
    max_diff = 2000
    INF = 10**9
    dp = {0: INF}
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        
        # For each existing state, we can add the current package

            new_diff = diff + (M_b * a - M_a * b)
            new_cost = cost + c
            if new_diff not in new_dp or new_dp[new_diff] > new_cost:
                new_dp[new_diff] = new_cost
```

 after more thought, I think the initial empty set should not be considered, and we should only consider states formed by at least one package.

 in the DP, we can start with no states, and for each package, we create a new state for that package alone, and for each existing state, we create a new state by adding this package.

 then after processing, for each diff=0, we take the min cost among all states that are formed by at least one package.

 in code, I can do:

```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # The difference we need is 0: M_b * sum_a = M_a * sum_b
    # For each package, the contribution to difference is diff_i = M_b * a_i - M_a * b_i
    # We want sum of diff_i = 0
    
    # DP: dp[diff] = minimum cost to achieve difference 'diff' using at least one package
    # Start with no states
    from collections import defaultdict
    dp = defaultdict(lambda: float('inf'))
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        
        # For each existing state, add this package
        for diff, cost in dp.items():
            new_diff = diff + (M_b * a - M_a * b)
            new_cost = cost + c
            if new_cost < new_dp[new_diff]:
                new_dp[new_diff] = new_cost
        
        # Also, add this package alone
        diff_i = M_b * a - M_a * b
        if c < new_dp[diff_i]:
            new_dp[diff_i] = c
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    if dp.get(0, float('inf')) == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
    else:
        print(dp[0])

solve()
```

Let me trace through the sample:
- Initially: dp = empty (defaultdict with inf)

Package 1: a=1,b=2,c=1, diff_i = 1*1 - 1*2 = -1
- new_dp = { -1: 1 }  (from single package)
- so after: dp = { -1: 1 }

Package 2: a=2,b=1,c=2, diff_i = 1*2 - 1*1 = 1
- new_dp = { -1: 1 } (copy)
- from diff=-1: new_diff = -1+1 = 0, new_cost = 1+2 = 3, so new_dp[0] = min(inf, 3) = 3
- from single package: new_dp[1] = min(inf, 2) = 2
- so after: dp = { -1:1, 0:3, 1:2 }

Package 3: a=3,b=3,c=10, diff_i = 0
- new_dp = { -1:1, 0:3, 1:2 }
- from each: 
  - from -1: new_diff = -1+0 = -1, cost=1+10=11 > 1, so no update
  - from 0: new_diff=0, cost=3+10=13 > 3, so no update
  - from 1: new_diff=1, cost=2+10=12 > 2, so no update
- from single package: diff_i=0, cost=10 > 3, so no update
- so dp remains { -1:1, 0:3, 1:2 }

Final: dp[0] = 3, so output 3. Correct.

Another sample: if only package 3, then after package 3: only state with diff=0 and cost=10, so output 10.

If only package 1: only state with diff=-1, cost=1, so for diff=0: inf, so output -1.

This seems correct.

So the algorithm is: use DP with states with at least one package, and for each package, update by adding to existing states and by adding alone.

We only consider combinations that have at least one package.

The cost for a state is the sum of the prices of the packages in the subset.

And we want the minimum cost for diff=0.

This should work within the constraints because the difference is bounded by at most 2000, and with 40 packages, the number of states is at most 2000, and for each package, we do 2000 work, so total 40 * 2000 = 80,000, which is acceptable.

So final code: 
```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # The difference we need is 0: M_b * sum_a = M_a * sum_b
    # For each package, the contribution to difference is diff_i = M_b * a_i - M_a * b_i
    # We want sum of diff_i = 0 for a non-empty subset
    from collections import defaultdict
    dp = defaultdict(lambda: float('inf'))
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        
        # For each existing state, add this package
        for diff, cost in dp.items():
            new_diff = diff + (M_b * a - M_a * b)
            new_cost = cost + c
            if new_cost < new_dp[new_diff]:
                new_dp[new_diff] = new_cost
        
        # Add this package alone
        diff_i = M_b * a - M_a * b
        if c < new_dp[diff_i]:
            new_dp[diff_i] = c
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    if dp.get(0, float('inf')) == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
    else:
        print(dp[0])

solve()
```

I think this is correct. Let me box it. 
Note: The bound on the difference is not exactly 2000, but since a_i, b_i <= 10, and M_a, M_b <= 10, the maximum |diff_i| is 10*10 + 10*10 = 200, and with 40 packages, max |sum| is 8000, so to be safe, we should set a bound.

But in the constraints, a_i, b_i <= 10, so max |M_b * a_i - M_a * b_i| <= 10*10 = 100, so max sum absolute is 40*100 = 4000.

We can set a bound of 4000, or in code, since we are only storing states that are reached, it will be only the ones that are actually formed.

In the worst case, it might be up to 4000, but 4000 is acceptable for a dictionary.

So the code should work. 
```python
def solve():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())

        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
``` 

I'll submit this. It passed the sample. 
Note: In the sample, when we add package 1 (diff=-1, cost=1), then package 2 (diff=1, cost=2), when we add package 2 to package 1, we get diff=0, cost=3, which is stored. Then when we add package 3, it doesn't change. So at the end, dp[0]=3. Correct. 
 If there is a solution with only one package, like if a package has diff_i=0, then when we add it alone, we set dp[0]=c, which is correct. 
 If no solution, dp[0] remains inf, so -1. 
 So it should be correct.

```python
from collections import defaultdict

def main():
    N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
    packages = []
    for _ in range(N):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        packages.append((a, b, c))
    
    # dp[diff] = minimum cost to achieve difference 'diff' with at least one package
    dp = defaultdict(lambda: float('inf'))
    
    for a, b, c in packages:
        new_dp = dp.copy()
        
        # Add this package to each existing state
        for diff, cost in dp.items():
            new_diff = diff + (M_b * a - M_a * b)
            new_cost = cost + c
            if new_cost < new_dp[new_diff]:
                new_dp[new_diff] = new_cost
        
        # Add this package alone
        diff_i = M_b * a - M_a * b
        if c < new_dp[diff_i]:
            new_dp[diff_i] = c
        
        dp = new_dp
    
    if dp.get(0, float('inf')) == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
    else:
        print(dp[0])

main()
```

Submitted Code

View Extracted Code 
from collections import defaultdict

N, M_a, M_b = map(int, input().split())
packages = []
for _ in range(N):
    a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
    packages.append((a, b, c))

# dp[diff] = minimum cost to achieve difference 'diff' with at least one package
dp = defaultdict(lambda: float('inf'))

for a, b, c in packages:
    new_dp = dp.copy()
    
    # Add this package to each existing state
    for diff, cost in dp.items():
        new_diff = diff + (M_b * a - M_a * b)
        new_cost = cost + c
        if new_cost < new_dp[new_diff]:
            new_dp[new_diff] = new_cost
    
    # Add this package alone
    diff_i = M_b * a - M_a * b
    if c < new_dp[diff_i]:
        new_dp[diff_i] = c
    
    dp = new_dp

if dp.get(0, float('inf')) == float('inf'):
    print(-1)
else:
    print(dp[0])

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Write a function with the signature shown below:
```python
def is_int_array(arr):
    return True
```
* returns `true  / True`  if every element in an array is an integer or a float with no decimals.
* returns `true  / True`  if array is empty.
* returns `false / False` for every other input.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step-by-step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to check if every element in an array is an integer or a float with no decimals
2. Returns True if:
   - Array is empty, OR
   - Every element is an integer or a float with no decimal part
3. Returns False for any other input

## Step-by-Step Analysis

Let me think about what constitutes "an integer or a float with no decimals":
- Integers: 1, 2, 3, 0, -1, -5
- Floats with no decimals: 1.0, 2.0, 3.0, 0.0, -1.0, -5.0
- Floats with decimals: 1.1, 2.3, 3.14, -0.5 → these should return False

## Key Observations

1. Empty array → True (as specified)
2. For non-empty arrays, we need to check each element
3. We need to distinguish between integers and floats with no decimal part
4. In Python, we can use `isinstance(x, int)` to check if it's an integer
5. For floats, we can check if `x.is_integer()` returns True (this works for floats like 1.0, 2.0, etc.)

## Algorithm

1. If the array is empty, return True
2. For each element in the array:
   - If it's not a number (not int or float), return False
   - If it's a float, check if it has no decimal part using `is_integer()`
   - If it's an int, it automatically has no decimal part
3. If all elements pass the check, return True

## Implementation

```python
def is_int_array(arr):
    # If array is empty, return True
    if not arr:
        return True
    
    # Check each element
    for element in arr:
        # Check if element is a number (int or float)
        if not isinstance(element, (int, float)):
            return False
        
        # If it's a float, check if it has no decimal part
        if isinstance(element, float) and not element.is_integer():
            return False
    
    return True
```

The solution works as follows:
- Empty array → returns True immediately
- For each element, we first verify it's either int or float
- If it's a float, we use the `.is_integer()` method which returns True only for floats like 1.0, 2.0, etc.
- Any other float (like 1.1, 2.3) will have `.is_integer()` return False
- Integers are automatically handled since they're already integers

This handles all the requirements: empty arrays, integers, floats with no decimals, and fails for any other input.

Let me break down what constitutes a valid element:

- Floats with no decimals: like 3.0, 2.0, -4.0, 0.0
- Invalid floats: like 3.1, 2.5, 4.01

- `isinstance(x, float)` and `x.is_integer()` - checks if it's a float with no decimal part

- Array with floats like 3.1, 2.5: should return False

## Step-by-Step Solution

- Integer: any whole number (like 5, -3, 0)
- Float with no decimals: any float that equals an integer (like 5.0, 3.0, -2.0)

### Step 2: Handle edge cases
- Empty array: should return True
- Array with mixed types: should return False
- Array with floats having decimals: should return False
- Array with non-numeric types: should return False

```python
    if not arr:
        return True
```

- `[1.1, 2.0, 3]` → False (1.1 has decimal)
- `[1, 2.5, 3]` → False (2.5 has decimal)
- `[1, "2", 3]` → False (string "2" is not a number)
- `[1.0, 2.0, 3.0]` → True (all floats with no decimals)

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Identify what makes a valid element
- Integers: 1, 2, 3, etc.
- Floats with no decimals: 3.0, 5.0, 10.0, etc.
- Invalid: 3.1, 4.5, 2.001, "5", [1,2], etc.

### Step 2: How to check if a float has no decimal part
- A float has no decimal part if `float_value == int(float_value)`
- For example: 3.0 == 3 (True), 3.1 == 3 (False)

### Step 3: Handle edge cases
- Empty array: should return True
- Array with mixed types: should return False
- Array with strings: should return False
- Array with complex numbers: should return False (though Python doesn't have complex numbers in basic types)

### Step 4: Algorithm
1. Check if array is empty → return True
2. For each element in the array:
   - Check if it's an integer (isinstance(x, int))
   - OR if it's a float and has no decimal part (isinstance(x, float) and x == int(x))
3. If all elements pass, return True; otherwise return False

### Step 5: Implementation

```python
def is_int_array(arr):

    if not arr:
        return True
    
    # Check each element
    for element in arr:
        # If element is not a number (int or float), return False

            return False
        
        # If it's a float, check if it has no decimal part
        if isinstance(element, float):
            if not element.is_integer():
                return False
    
    return True
```

- An integer can be represented as a float with no decimal part (e.g., 3.0)
- We need to check that every element is either an integer type or a float with zero decimal places
- If any element is a float with decimal places (like 3.5), or a string, or any other type, return False

- `isinstance(element, (int, float))` ensures the element is either an integer or float

- If any element fails these checks, we return False immediately
- If all elements pass, we return True

This solution handles all the required cases:
- Empty array → True
- Array with integers → True
- Array with floats with no decimals → True  
- Array with floats with decimals → False

        return True
    
    # Check each element in the array
    for element in arr:
        # Check if element is an integer or a float with no decimal part
        if not isinstance(element, (int, float)):
            return False
        
        # If it's a float, check if it has no decimal part
        if isinstance(element, float) and not element.is_integer():
            return False
    
    return True
